Now, the coalescer is applied top-down. `map_top_down` exists. But there's a catch: `map_top_down` applies `f` to the root, then recurses into
children of the *result*. If we transform a Choice into CharClass, children are gone — good. If we transform `Seq(NegPred(Choice), ANY)` into
NegCharClass, children gone — good. If a Choice has a qualifying run but leaves non-qualifying alternatives, the result is still a Choice, so
map_top_down recurses into its children (which includes CharClass? no, CharClass has no children to recurse). The non-qualifying children remain as
leaves or nested Choices. Fine.

But there's a subtle issue: when we use `map_top_down`, the function is applied to the top-level `Choice` first. If the top-level Choice has some
qualifying run and we return a rebuilt Choice where the run is replaced by CharClass, then map_top_down recurses into children. The non-qualifying
alternatives that are themselves `Choice` nodes (nested subchoices) will be processed by `f` later. That's top-down. Good.

But wait: there's a subtlety about the order of processing a `Choice` node. In `map_top_down`, after `f(expr)` returns, it matches the returned expr.
If it's `Choice(lhs, rhs)`, it recurses into both children with `f`. So if our `f` returns the original `Choice` unchanged (because no coalescing), it
will recurse into lhs and rhs. That means the nested structure will also be coalesced. Good.

use crate::ast::*;

pub fn rotate(rule: Rule) -> Rule {
    fn rotate_internal(expr: Expr) -> Expr {
        match expr {
            Expr::Seq(lhs, rhs) => {
                let lhs = *lhs;
                match lhs {
                    Expr::Seq(ll, lr) => {
                        rotate_internal(Expr::Seq(ll, Box::new(Expr::Seq(lr, rhs))))
                    }
                    lhs => Expr::Seq(Box::new(lhs), rhs),
                }
            }
            Expr::Choice(lhs, rhs) => {
                let lhs = *lhs;
                match lhs {
                    Expr::Choice(ll, lr) => {
                        rotate_internal(Expr::Choice(ll, Box::new(Expr::Choice(lr, rhs))))
                    }
                    lhs => Expr::Choice(Box::new(lhs), rhs),
                }
            }
            expr => expr,
        }
    }

    let Rule { name, ty, expr } = rule;
    Rule {
        name,
        ty,
        expr: expr.map_top_down(rotate_internal),
    }
}

REASONING_CONTENT
Rotator turns left-nested choices into right-nested. So a flat chain `a | b | c` is `Choice(a, Choice(b, c))`. A nested parenthesized choice `(a | b)
| c` would be `Choice(Choice(a,b), Choice(c,...))`? Let's think: original AST for `(a|b) | c` is `Choice(Choice(a,b), c)`. Rotator sees lhs =
Choice(a,b), recurses: `rotate_internal(Choice(Choice(a,b), c))` => lhs is Choice(a,b), so `rotate_internal(Choice(a, Choice(b, c)))` = `Choice(a,
Choice(b,c))`. So rotator actually flattens nested left choices too. Interesting: any left-nested Choice gets flattened. What about `a | (b | c)`?
Original is `Choice(a, Choice(b,c))`; rotator: lhs = a (not Choice), returns `Choice(a, Choice(b,c))`. So all choice chains are right-nested in
OptimizedExpr.

So when coalescing, flattening by following `rhs` while `Choice(lhs,rhs)` is correct.

But what about a parenthesized choice used as a single alternative among non-choice items, e.g., `a | (b | c) | d` — that's just a flat chain `a | b |
c | d` in the AST. There's no notion of grouping; pest treats `(b|c)` as a subexpression but rotator flattens it. So the whole chain is flat.

Now, for the NegCharClass transformation: `NegPred(Choice(lhs, rhs)) ~ ANY`. The choice inner is a flat chain. We need to check all alternatives
qualify. Good.

Now, let's consider the "fewer ranges than original alternative count" gate for partial runs. Suppose a chain has alternatives: [Str('a'), Ident(x),
Str('b'), Str('c'), Str('d')]. Qualifying run 2 = [b,c,d] (3 alternatives). Merge ranges [b,b],[c,c],[d,d] -> [b-d] = 1 range < 3, so replace run with
Range('b','d'). Result: `Str('a') | Ident(x) | Range('b','d')`. Good.

Suppose run [Str('a'), Str('b'), Str('x')] -> merge [a,a],[b,b],[x,x] = 3 ranges = 3 alternatives, not fewer, so leave unchanged.

Now, what about "A coalesced result is emitted only when merging produces fewer ranges than the original alternative count" — for a full chain of 2,
e.g. `Str('a') | Str('b')` -> 1 range < 2, emit Range. But "When only some qualify, contiguous runs of three or more" — full chain qualifies, so the
3+ rule doesn't apply? Hmm, but maybe they intend 3+ universally. Let me look at the wording once more: "Choice chains of qualifying alternatives
collapse into CharClass holding merged character ranges." This is the main rule, no count. "When only some qualify, contiguous runs of three or more
qualifying alternatives are coalesced." This adds a restriction specifically for partial. So full chains don't have the 3+ restriction. But is a
2-alternative full chain "a choice chain"? Yes. I'll implement full-chain coalescing regardless of count (gated by fewer ranges), and partial runs
require >=3.

Wait, but there's a scenario: `a | b` (2 single chars) — is it beneficial? It produces 1 Range node instead of 2 Str nodes and a Choice. Yes, fewer
ranges. This seems intended by "A single merged range simplifies to Range when endpoints differ or Str when equal." That sentence explicitly covers
the result of merging possibly 2 alternatives into a single Range/Str. So yes, 2 can coalesce.

Now, another important consideration: The gate "merging produces fewer ranges than the original alternative count" should compare against the count of
qualifying alternatives in the run, or total original count? For a full chain, both are same. For partial runs, the run's alternative count is the
relevant count. If run of 3 produces 2 ranges, replace with CharClass (2 ranges), leaving total node count = 1 + (non-qual count). I'll use run count.

Now, another question: When all alternatives qualify but merging produces fewer ranges than count, we produce CharClass. But what if some alternatives
are multi-char Str? Then they don't qualify, so it's partial. Good.

Now, let's think about case-insensitive expansion details. `^"a"` should match 'a' and 'A'. So ranges [a,a] and [A,A]. If alternatives are `^"a" |
^"A"`, both expand to same ranges -> merge to [A-a]? Sorted: [A,A], [a,a]. They are adjacent? A=65, a=97, gap of 31, not adjacent, so result
[A,A],[a,a] = 2 ranges, same as 2 alternatives -> not emitted. Fine. `^"a" | "b"` -> ranges [A,A],[a,a],[b,b] = 3 ranges, alternatives 2 -> not
emitted (3 > 2). Hmm, but could we merge [a,b]? The lower 'A' is separate. [a,b] merges into [a-b] (since a=97, b=98 adjacent). So ranges become
[A,A], [a-b] = 2 ranges, alternatives 2 -> not fewer, not emitted. Good.

Now, what does "alphabetic characters" mean for Insens? `^"é"` — Insens is ASCII-only per docs, so matching uses `eq_ignore_ascii_case`, which treats
é as non-alphabetic and only matches exact é. So we only expand ASCII alphabetic. Use `c.is_ascii_alphabetic()`. Then add both `to_ascii_lowercase()`
and `to_ascii_uppercase()`. Actually if c is already uppercase 'A', lower is 'a', upper is 'A'. Both included. If c is lowercase 'a', both included.
If c is '1', only [1,1]. Good.

Now, let's think about how to represent ranges as `(String, String)` with chars. For Unicode chars like 'é', `String::from('é')` works. For start/end
of a Range, they're strings from grammar (single char). We'll convert to char and back. Need to handle invalid (multi-char) strings in Range? Grammar
ensures single char. But for robustness, use `.chars().next().expect(...)`. Existing code does that.

Now, implementation of merging in Rust:

```rust
fn merge_ranges(ranges: Vec<(char, char)>) -> Vec<(char, char)> {
    let mut ranges = ranges;
    ranges.sort_by_key(|(start, _)| *start as u32);
    let mut merged: Vec<(char, char)> = Vec::new();
    for (start, end) in ranges {
        if let Some((_, last_end)) = merged.last_mut() {
            if (*last_end as u32) + 1 >= start as u32 {
                if end > *last_end {
                    *last_end = end;
                }
                continue;
            }
        }
        merged.push((start, end));
    }
    merged
}
```

Need to guard `last_end as u32 + 1` overflow: max char is 0x10FFFF, +1 = 0x110000 fits u32. No overflow. Good.

But there's a subtlety: sorting by start only; if two ranges have same start but different ends, sorted by start only is unstable but merge handles
max end. Fine.

Now, `qualifying ranges`:

```rust
fn ranges_of(expr: &OptimizedExpr) -> Option<Vec<(char, char)>> {
    match expr {
        OptimizedExpr::Str(s) if s.chars().count() == 1 => Some(vec![(c, c)]),
        OptimizedExpr::Insens(s) if s.chars().count() == 1 => {
            let c = s.chars().next().unwrap();
            if c.is_ascii_alphabetic() {
                Some(vec![(lower, lower), (upper, upper)])
            } else {
                Some(vec![(c, c)])
            }
        }
        OptimizedExpr::Range(start, end) => {
            let start = start.chars().next()?;
            let end = end.chars().next()?;
            Some(vec![(start, end)])
        }
        OptimizedExpr::CharClass(ranges) => Some(ranges.iter().map(|(s,e)| (start_char, end_char)).collect()),
        OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(inner) => ranges_of(inner),
        _ => None,
    }
}
```

Should we require `Range` endpoints valid single chars? If start or end empty or multi-char, return None (not qualify). Existing Range is always
single char from grammar, but robust.

For `Str`, `s.chars().count() == 1` handles Unicode char length. `s.len()` would count bytes, so use chars().count().

Now, building result from merged ranges:

```rust
fn build_char_expr(ranges: Vec<(char,char)>) -> Option<OptimizedExpr> {
    if ranges.is_empty() { return None; }
    if ranges.len() == 1 {
        let (start, end) = ranges[0];
        if start == end {
            Some(OptimizedExpr::Str(start.to_string()))
        } else {
            Some(OptimizedExpr::Range(start.to_string(), end.to_string()))
        }
    } else {
        Some(OptimizedExpr::CharClass(ranges.iter().map(|(s,e)| (s.to_string(), e.to_string())).collect()))
    }
}
```

Now the coalescing of a Choice:

```rust
fn coalesce_choice(lhs: Box<OptimizedExpr>, rhs: Box<OptimizedExpr>) -> OptimizedExpr {
    // flatten
    let mut alternatives: Vec<OptimizedExpr> = Vec::new();
    let mut current = OptimizedExpr::Choice(lhs, rhs);
    while let OptimizedExpr::Choice(l, r) = current {
        alternatives.push(*l);
        current = *r;
    }
    alternatives.push(current);

    // Determine which qualify and their ranges
    let qualified: Vec<Option<Vec<(char,char)>>> = alternatives.iter().map(ranges_of).collect();

    // Find runs
    let mut result: Vec<OptimizedExpr> = Vec::new();
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < alternatives.len() {
        if qualified[i].is_some() {
            // find run end
            let mut j = i;
            while j < alternatives.len() && qualified[j].is_some() { j += 1; }
            let run_len = j - i;
            let is_full_chain = i == 0 && j == alternatives.len();
            let run_ranges: Vec<(char,char)> = (i..j).flat_map(|k| qualified[k].clone().unwrap()).collect();
            let merged = merge_ranges(run_ranges);
            let should_coalesce = if is_full_chain {
                merged.len() < run_len
            } else {
                run_len >= 3 && merged.len() < run_len
            };
            if should_coalesce {
                if let Some(expr) = build_char_expr(merged) {
                    result.push(expr);
                } else {
                    // shouldn't happen
                    result.extend(alternatives[i..j].iter().cloned());
                }
            } else {
                result.extend(alternatives[i..j].iter().cloned());
            }
            i = j;
        } else {
            result.push(alternatives[i].clone());
            i += 1;
        }
    }

    // rebuild right-associative choice
    if result.len() == 1 {
        result.pop().unwrap()
    } else {
        let mut iter = result.into_iter().rev();
        let mut acc = iter.next().unwrap();
        for alt in iter {
            acc = OptimizedExpr::Choice(Box::new(alt), Box::new(acc));
        }
        acc
    }
}
```

Wait, there's a subtle issue with `is_full_chain`: if all alternatives qualify, `i==0 && j==len`. But what if there are multiple runs that
collectively cover all alternatives? That's impossible if all qualify — it's one run. So full chain means all qualify. Good.

But there's another subtle issue: what if all qualify but run_len == 1? A Choice with a single alternative shouldn't happen (Choice always has 2
children), but after flatten, len >= 2. So run_len >= 2 for full chain.

Now, for partial run, do we also need to apply the "fewer ranges" gate? Yes.

Now, for the NegCharClass transformation:

```rust
fn coalesce_seq(lhs, rhs) -> OptimizedExpr {
    if let OptimizedExpr::NegPred(inner) = *lhs {
        if let OptimizedExpr::Ident(name) = *rhs {
            if name == "ANY" {
                // collect alternatives from inner (if Choice, flatten; else single)
                if let Some(ranges) = qualifying_ranges_for_neg(*inner) {
                    let merged = merge_ranges(ranges);
                    return OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(merged as strings);
                }
            }
        }
    }
    OptimizedExpr::Seq(lhs, rhs)
}
```

```rust
fn neg_ranges(expr: OptimizedExpr) -> Option<Vec<(char,char)>> {
    let mut alternatives = vec![];
    let mut current = expr;
    while let OptimizedExpr::Choice(l, r) = current {
        alternatives.push(*l);
        current = *r;
    }
    alternatives.push(current);
    let mut ranges = vec![];
    for alt in &alternatives {
        ranges.extend(ranges_of(alt)?);
    }
    Some(ranges)
}
```

REASONING_CONTENT
`map_top_down` on OptimizedExpr is only used in `mod.rs` (the public method) and `restorer` uses `map_bottom_up`. Neither recurses into RestoreOnErr.
That's a pre-existing limitation.

For the coalescer, the spec says "applied top-down". The simplest is to use `map_top_down`. But that won't traverse into `RestoreOnErr` wrappers.
Should we modify `map_top_down`/`map_bottom_up`/`iter_top_down` to recurse into RestoreOnErr? That could change behavior of `restorer` (map_bottom_up
currently doesn't recurse into RestoreOnErr, so restorer won't re-wrap inside). If we add recursion into RestoreOnErr in map_bottom_up, then
`restorer` would process the inner of a RestoreOnErr and potentially wrap it again, causing infinite nesting? Let's think: `restorer` calls
`expr.map_bottom_up(|expr| wrap_branching_exprs(expr, rules))`. If map_bottom_up recursed into RestoreOnErr, then for `RestoreOnErr(Opt(expr))`, it
would process inner Opt first, potentially wrap it in RestoreOnErr if modifies state -> `RestoreOnErr(Opt(RestoreOnErr(expr)))`? Actually
wrap_branching_exprs on `Opt(expr)` returns `Opt(RestoreOnErr(expr))` if modifies. Then outer RestoreOnErr stays. That would double-wrap. So modifying
map_bottom_up is risky.

Better: write the coalescer with its own top-down traversal that handles RestoreOnErr by recursing into it (and preserving the wrapper). Or, since
coalescing is about Choice/Seq at any level, and RestoreOnErr can contain nested Choices, we should recurse into RestoreOnErr. Let's implement our own
`coalesce_expr_top_down` recursive function rather than relying on `map_top_down`. Or we can use `map_top_down` and accept it doesn't recurse into
RestoreOnErr — but that would fail if tests have `Opt(Choice(...))` where the Choice is wrapped in RestoreOnErr. However, such tests might be
constructed manually. The spec explicitly says "A RestoreOnErr-wrapped alternative qualifies when its inner expression qualifies" — this refers to
alternatives in a Choice, which we handle in `coalesce_choice` directly (we call `ranges_of` on `RestoreOnErr(inner)`). It doesn't require traversing
into RestoreOnErr containers. But to be safe and robust, I'll write our own recursive traversal that handles all containers including RestoreOnErr.

Let me design `coalesce_rule`:

```rust
pub fn coalesce(rule: OptimizedRule) -> OptimizedRule {
    OptimizedRule {
        name: rule.name,
        ty: rule.ty,
        expr: coalesce_expr(rule.expr),
    }
}

fn coalesce_expr(expr: OptimizedExpr) -> OptimizedExpr {
    match expr {
        OptimizedExpr::Choice(lhs, rhs) => {
            coalesce_choice(*lhs, *rhs)
        }
        OptimizedExpr::Seq(lhs, rhs) => {
            coalesce_seq(*lhs, *rhs)
        }
        OptimizedExpr::PosPred(inner) => OptimizedExpr::PosPred(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner))),
        OptimizedExpr::NegPred(inner) => OptimizedExpr::NegPred(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner))),
        OptimizedExpr::Rep(inner) => OptimizedExpr::Rep(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner))),
        OptimizedExpr::Opt(inner) => OptimizedExpr::Opt(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner))),
        OptimizedExpr::Push(inner) => OptimizedExpr::Push(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner))),
        #[cfg(feature = "grammar-extras")]
        OptimizedExpr::RepOnce(inner) => OptimizedExpr::RepOnce(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner))),
        #[cfg(feature = "grammar-extras")]
        OptimizedExpr::NodeTag(inner, tag) => OptimizedExpr::NodeTag(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner)), tag),
        OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(inner) => OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner))),
        expr => expr,
    }
}
```

This is basically a top-down map that recurses into RestoreOnErr. Then in `coalesce_choice`, after we rebuild the choice with coalesced runs, do we
need to recurse into the remaining alternatives? Since `coalesce_expr` calls `coalesce_choice` and then returns the result directly (not recursing
into children), we need `coalesce_choice` to also recurse into non-coalesced alternatives (and nested choices). Hmm. The spec says "applied top-down"
— meaning we process each node from top down. In a recursive function, if `coalesce_expr` on a Choice just calls `coalesce_choice` which flattens and
coalesces runs but doesn't recurse into the remaining nested Choice children, then nested choices inside alternatives (e.g., a non-qualifying
alternative that is itself a Choice) wouldn't be coalesced.

Let's think: Can a non-qualifying alternative be a Choice? In a flat right-nested choice chain, all alternatives are either leaves or nested Choice
nodes? Actually rotator flattens left-nested choices, so `(a | b) | c` becomes flat `a | b | c`. But a non-qualifying alternative could be a Choice if
it's parenthesized on the right: `a | (b | c) | d` — but rotator also flattens that because it's `Choice(a, Choice(Choice(b,c), d))`? Let's trace:
original `a | (b | c) | d` parses as `Choice(a, Choice(Choice(b,c), d))` (assuming left-assoc parsing). Rotator on `Choice(a,
rhs=Choice(Choice(b,c),d))`: lhs=a not Choice, so returns `Choice(a, rhs)` unchanged? Wait rotate_internal is applied top-down. The outer Choice's lhs
is `a`, so it returns `Choice(a, rhs)` where rhs is still `Choice(Choice(b,c), d)`. Then map_top_down recurses into rhs (a Choice): lhs=Choice(b,c),
so it flattens to `Choice(b, Choice(c,d))`. So the final tree is `Choice(a, Choice(b, Choice(c,d)))` — fully flat. So no nested Choice alternatives
remain after rotator in a flat chain? Actually what about a nested choice used as a sequence element: `(a|b) ~ c`? That's inside Seq, and rotator
handles Seq but not Choice inside Seq specially — `Seq(Choice(a,b), c)` stays. The Choice is a child of Seq, and coalesce_expr will process it when
recursing into Seq children.

So in a flat Choice chain, after rotator, all alternatives are non-Choice leaves. But what about a choice that's a child of a non-qualifying
alternative? E.g., an alternative could be `Opt(Choice(...))` — not qualifying, and its inner Choice should be coalesced. If `coalesce_choice` doesn't
recurse into non-qualifying alternatives, the inner Choice in `Opt(Choice(...))` won't be processed. But if we use `map_top_down`, it would recurse
into those children after the top-level Choice is processed.

So I need to recurse into the remaining alternatives after coalescing. Let me make `coalesce_choice` recursively process each alternative:

```rust
fn coalesce_choice(lhs: Box<OptimizedExpr>, rhs: Box<OptimizedExpr>) -> OptimizedExpr {
    // flatten original
    let alternatives = flatten_choice(lhs, rhs);
    // process each alternative top-down FIRST? or process runs?
```

Actually, the "top-down" approach means: first try to coalesce the current Choice node based on its current children (before children are coalesced).
Then recurse into the children that remain. So in `coalesce_expr` for Choice, we should:
1. Flatten the current chain.
2. Identify runs using the current children's qualification.
3. Coalesce runs where beneficial.
4. Rebuild the Choice.
5. Recurse into each alternative of the rebuilt Choice (applying `coalesce_expr`).

But wait, if we recurse into the rebuilt alternatives, a child that was itself a Choice (if any) gets processed. Also, leaf alternatives like
Str/Range don't need processing. And the CharClass/Range/Str produced from a run don't need processing.

However, there's a subtle issue: after coalescing a run, the rebuilt Choice may have fewer alternatives. Recurse into each.

But also, after we recurse into children, could a child Choice merge with siblings? No, because the child is a separate alternative within a Seq or
other structure, not part of the same Choice chain (after flatten, there are no Choice children in a pure flat chain, but there could be in a non-flat
structure like `Seq(Choice(...), ...)` — those are processed as part of their parent Seq, not as alternatives of the current Choice). Actually within
a flat Choice chain, alternatives are leaves, so no child Choices. But after `coalesce_choice` rebuilds, all remaining alternatives are leaves (since
all non-coalesced qualifying leaves stay, non-qualifying leaves stay). So no recursion needed for the rebuilt Choice's children except to handle
wrappers like `Opt(Choice)` which are leaves of the chain but contain nested Choices. Those should be recursively coalesced.

Let me implement `coalesce_choice` as:
1. Flatten current chain into alternatives (raw).
2. For each alternative, apply `coalesce_expr` to it first? Or after?

Hmm. "Applied top-down" means process current node before children. For the choice node, the decision of whether an alternative qualifies should be
based on the *current* alternative (before its children are processed). But for a leaf like `Str`, `Range`, `CharClass`, `RestoreOnErr(Str)` it
doesn't matter. For an alternative like `Opt(Choice(...))`, it doesn't qualify regardless. For an alternative that is a `Choice` (if possible), it
doesn't qualify (since Choice isn't a qualifying leaf), but after processing it internally it might become CharClass... but it was a separate
alternative in the chain — after rotator that shouldn't happen. So the order doesn't matter much.

I'll do:
1. Flatten raw alternatives.
2. Coalesce runs using raw qualification -> produce a new Vec<OptimizedExpr> (some runs replaced).
3. Recurse into each element of the new Vec (i.e., apply coalesce_expr to each remaining alternative).
4. Rebuild right-assoc choice from the processed Vec.

But wait, if we recurse into a run-replacement (Range/Str/CharClass), nothing happens. If we recurse into a non-qualifying alternative, its internal
choices get coalesced. Good.

But there's a subtlety: when recursing into the alternatives after rebuilding, a nested Choice alternative could potentially become a qualifying leaf
(e.g., `Opt(Choice(...))` won't; but `RestoreOnErr(Choice(...))`? After coalesce_expr recurses into RestoreOnErr, the inner Choice might become a
Range/Str/CharClass, making the whole RestoreOnErr(Range) a qualifying leaf. But at that point, it's already in the rebuilt Choice; we won't re-check
runs with it. That's acceptable — top-down means we don't re-run the same node's coalescing after children change. In practice, nested choices inside
a RestoreOnErr alternative of a Choice chain are unusual.

Actually, a cleaner approach: use `map_top_down` but with our own recursive variant that includes RestoreOnErr. We can write `coalesce_expr` as a
recursive function that:
- For Choice: flatten, coalesce runs, rebuild, then recursively call `coalesce_expr` on each alternative in the rebuilt choice.
- For Seq: check NegPred-ANY transformation; if not transformed, recurse into lhs/rhs.
- For other containers: recurse.

This matches top-down. Let me implement that.

Now, there's another subtlety: after coalescing a full chain into a single Range/Str/CharClass, we return that (no recursion needed). For partial, we
recurse into remaining alternatives.

Let me also consider: `coalesce_choice` should handle the case where the result has a single element (e.g., all alternatives coalesced into one
Range). Return that directly.

Now, the NegCharClass: `coalesce_seq` should recursively process the children when not transformed. So:

```rust
fn coalesce_seq(lhs: Box<OptimizedExpr>, rhs: Box<OptimizedExpr>) -> OptimizedExpr {
    if let OptimizedExpr::NegPred(inner) = lhs.as_ref() {
        if let OptimizedExpr::Ident(name) = rhs.as_ref() {
            if name == "ANY" {
                if let Some(ranges) = collect_ranges_from_neg(inner) {
                    let merged = merge_ranges(ranges);
                    if !merged.is_empty() {
                        return OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(to_string_ranges(merged));
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }
    OptimizedExpr::Seq(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*lhs)), Box::new(coalesce_expr(*rhs)))
}
```

Wait — if we call `coalesce_expr(*lhs)` on the NegPred child, and the inner choice had a qualifying run, it might become Range/Str/CharClass. But we
already tried the NegPred-ANY transformation on the raw inner; if it failed because not all alternatives qualified, then recursing into lhs would
coalesce partial runs in the inner choice, and then the outer Seq would no longer be `NegPred(Choice) ~ ANY`. That's fine; the spec only transforms
when all qualify.

But there's a subtlety: if the inner is `NegPred(Range)` (single qualifying alternative), we transform to NegCharClass. Good. If inner is
`NegPred(CharClass)`, also transform. If inner is `NegPred(RestoreOnErr(Range))`, also transform.

Now, let's think about `collect_ranges_from_neg(inner: &OptimizedExpr) -> Option<Vec<(char,char)>>`:
- Flatten inner if Choice.
- For each alt, call `ranges_of(alt)`; if any None, return None.
- Collect all ranges.

Now, let's think about whether the NegCharClass transformation should also consider `RestoreOnErr` wrapping the whole `NegPred` or the `ANY`? E.g.
`Seq(RestoreOnErr(NegPred(Choice)), ANY)`? The spec says "A negated predicate over qualifying alternatives followed by ANY". I think just
`NegPred(...) ~ ANY`. But we could also handle `RestoreOnErr(NegPred(Choice)) ~ ANY` by looking through RestoreOnErr. Since RestoreOnErr is
semantically transparent, it might be worth handling. But the restorer wouldn't wrap a NegPred in RestoreOnErr (NegPred isn't a branch, Opt, Rep). So
skip.

Now, let's also think about whether the `CharClass` variant's ranges should be sorted/merged when constructed by the coalescer — yes. And when
absorbing an existing CharClass's ranges, they might be unsorted/unmerged? The coalescer always produces merged/sorted CharClass, so any CharClass in
input is already merged/sorted. But for safety, we'll merge the union anyway.

Now, let's also consider the Display and Debug. `CharClass` and `NegCharClass` derive Clone/Debug/Eq/PartialEq automatically. Good.

Now, let's check the generator's `generate_expr` for Range: it does `start.chars().next().unwrap()` and then `quote! { state.match_range(#start..#end)
}`. For CharClass, we need to generate a call to `match_char_class`. We'll write helper functions in generator to build the token stream for ranges.

    34	/// A helper that will unwrap the result or panic
    35	/// with the nicely formatted error message.
    36	pub fn unwrap_or_report<T, E>(result: Result<T, E>) -> T
    37	where
    38	    E: IntoIterator,
    39	    E::Item: Display,
    40	{
    41	    result.unwrap_or_else(|e| {
    42	        panic!(
    43	            "{}{}",
    44	            "grammar error\n\n".to_owned(),
    45	            &e.into_iter()
    46	                .map(|error| format!("{}", error))

    49	        )
    50	    })

    53	/// A tuple returned by the validation and processing of the parsed grammar.
    54	/// The first element is the vector of used builtin rule names,
    55	/// the second element is the vector of optimized rules.
    56	type UsedBuiltinAndOptimized<'i> = (Vec<&'i str>, Vec<optimizer::OptimizedRule>);
    57	
    58	/// Parses, validates, processes and optimizes the provided grammar.
    59	pub fn parse_and_optimize(
    60	    grammar: &str,
    61	) -> Result<UsedBuiltinAndOptimized<'_>, Vec<Error<parser::Rule>>> {
    62	    let pairs = match parser::parse(parser::Rule::grammar_rules, grammar) {
    63	        Ok(pairs) => Ok(pairs),
    64	        Err(error) => Err(vec![error]),
    65	    }?;
    66	
    67	    let defaults = validator::validate_pairs(pairs.clone())?;
    68	    let ast = parser::consume_rules(pairs)?;
    69	
    70	    Ok((defaults, optimizer::optimize(ast)))
    71	}

First, add the new variants to `OptimizedExpr` in `meta/src/optimizer/mod.rs`. Then add Display. Then add the coalescer module and wire it into
`optimize()`.

Let me make the edits.

First, add variants after Range (or after Insens). I'll add:
```rust
/// Matches a single character from a set of ranges, e.g. `'a'..'z' | '0'..'9'`
CharClass(Vec<(String, String)>),
/// Matches a single character not in any of the ranges, e.g. `!('a'..'z') ~ ANY`
NegCharClass(Vec<(String, String)>),
```

Add docs.

Now, the Display:
```rust
OptimizedExpr::CharClass(ranges) => {
    let ranges = ranges.iter().map(|(start, end)| {
        let start = start.chars().next().expect("Empty range start.");
        let end = end.chars().next().expect("Empty range end.");
        format!("({:?}..{:?})", start, end)
    }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" | ");
    write!(f, "({})", ranges)
}
OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(ranges) => {
    let ranges = ...same...;
    write!(f, "!({}) ~ ANY", ranges)
}
```

Actually for consistency with Range which writes `('a'..'z')`, I'll write CharClass as `('a'..'z' | '0'..'9')` and NegCharClass as `!('a'..'z' |
'0'..'9') ~ ANY`.

Now, the coalescer module. Let me write `meta/src/optimizer/coalescer.rs`.

Let me think through details of `ranges_of` for Str with single char. `s.chars().count() == 1`. For Insens, same. For Range, endpoints may be empty or
multi-char; use `chars().next()` and require that both endpoints have exactly one char? Since grammar ensures single char, but to be safe, return None
if `start.chars().count() != 1 || end.chars().count() != 1`. Actually Range with a single char is fine. Let me use `.chars().next()?` and check
`count() == 1`.

For CharClass, each range must have single char endpoints; if any malformed, return None (treat as not qualifying) or skip? Better return None for the
whole alternative if malformed. But since coalescer produces well-formed, fine.

Now, `merge_ranges`:
- Input Vec<(char,char)>.
- Sort by start char code point.
- Merge overlapping/adjacent.
- Return Vec<(char,char)>.

Now, `build_char_expr(ranges)`.

Now, `coalesce_choice`.

Let me write it carefully.

```rust
fn flatten_choice(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> Vec<OptimizedExpr> {
    let mut alternatives = vec![lhs];
    let mut current = rhs;
    while let OptimizedExpr::Choice(l, r) = current {
        alternatives.push(*l);
        current = *r;
    }
    alternatives.push(current);
    alternatives
}
```

Wait, careful: the initial call `coalesce_choice(lhs, rhs)` receives `lhs` and `rhs` as `Box<OptimizedExpr>` after deref. So signature could be `fn
coalesce_choice(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> OptimizedExpr`. Let me define that.

Flatten:
```rust
fn flatten_choice(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> Vec<OptimizedExpr> {
    let mut alternatives = vec![lhs];
    let mut current = rhs;
    while let OptimizedExpr::Choice(l, r) = current {
        alternatives.push(*l);
        current = *r;
    }
    alternatives.push(current);
    alternatives
}
```

Then:
```rust
fn coalesce_choice(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> OptimizedExpr {
    let alternatives = flatten_choice(lhs, rhs);
    let qualified: Vec<Option<Vec<(char, char)>>> = alternatives.iter().map(ranges_of).collect();

    let mut result: Vec<OptimizedExpr> = Vec::new();
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < alternatives.len() {
        if qualified[i].is_some() {
            let mut j = i;
            while j < alternatives.len() && qualified[j].is_some() {
                j += 1;
            }
            let run_len = j - i;
            let all_qualified = i == 0 && j == alternatives.len();
            let ranges: Vec<(char, char)> = (i..j)
                .flat_map(|k| qualified[k].as_ref().unwrap().iter().copied())
                .collect();
            let merged = merge_ranges(ranges);
            let coalesce = if all_qualified {
                merged.len() < run_len
            } else {
                run_len >= 3 && merged.len() < run_len
            };
            if coalesce {
                if let Some(expr) = build_char_expr(merged) {
                    result.push(expr);
                } else {
                    result.extend(alternatives[i..j].iter().cloned());
                }
            } else {
                result.extend(alternatives[i..j].iter().cloned());
            }
            i = j;
        } else {
            result.push(alternatives[i].clone());
            i += 1;
        }
    }

    // Recursively coalesce remaining alternatives.
    let result = result.into_iter().map(coalesce_expr).collect::<Vec<_>>();

    rebuild_choice(result)
}
```

Wait, but there's a subtle issue: if a run is not coalesced (because merged.len() >= run_len), we push the raw alternatives. Then we recurse into them
via `coalesce_expr`. That's fine.

But there's another subtle issue: For the `all_qualified` case, `run_len` is the total number of alternatives. If `merged.len() < run_len`, we
coalesce into a single expr (or CharClass). Good. But what if `run_len == 1`? Can't happen for a Choice (min 2). For `all_qualified`, if `run_len ==
2` and merged.len() == 2, no coalesce. If merged.len() == 1, coalesce to Range/Str.

Now, `rebuild_choice`:
```rust
fn rebuild_choice(alternatives: Vec<OptimizedExpr>) -> OptimizedExpr {
    let mut iter = alternatives.into_iter();
    let first = iter.next().expect("non-empty choice");
    let mut acc = first;
    for alt in iter {
        acc = OptimizedExpr::Choice(Box::new(acc), Box::new(alt));
    }
    acc
}
```

Wait, if we have alternatives [a,b,c], we want `Choice(a, Choice(b,c))`. The above builds: acc=a; then Choice(a,b); then Choice(Choice(a,b), c).
That's left-nested! Wrong. Need right-nested: iterate in reverse.

```rust
fn rebuild_choice(alternatives: Vec<OptimizedExpr>) -> OptimizedExpr {
    let mut iter = alternatives.into_iter().rev();
    let mut acc = iter.next().expect("non-empty choice");
    for alt in iter {
        acc = OptimizedExpr::Choice(Box::new(alt), Box::new(acc));
    }
    acc
}
```
For [a,b,c]: reverse c,b,a; acc=c; alt=b -> Choice(b,c); alt=a -> Choice(a, Choice(b,c)). Correct.

Now, `coalesce_seq`:
```rust
fn coalesce_seq(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> OptimizedExpr {
    if let OptimizedExpr::NegPred(inner) = &lhs {
        if let OptimizedExpr::Ident(name) = &rhs {
            if name == "ANY" {
                if let Some(ranges) = collect_neg_ranges(inner) {
                    let merged = merge_ranges(ranges);
                    if !merged.is_empty() {
                        return OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(
                            merged.into_iter().map(to_string_range).collect()
                        );
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }
    OptimizedExpr::Seq(Box::new(coalesce_expr(lhs)), Box::new(coalesce_expr(rhs)))
}
```

`collect_neg_ranges(expr: &OptimizedExpr) -> Option<Vec<(char,char)>>`:
```rust
fn collect_neg_ranges(expr: &OptimizedExpr) -> Option<Vec<(char, char)>> {
    let mut alternatives = vec![];
    let mut current = expr;
    while let OptimizedExpr::Choice(l, r) = current {
        alternatives.push(l.as_ref());
        current = r.as_ref();
    }
    alternatives.push(current);
    let mut ranges = vec![];
    for alt in alternatives {
        ranges.extend(ranges_of(alt)?);
    }
    Some(ranges)
}
```

Now, `coalesce_expr`:
```rust
fn coalesce_expr(expr: OptimizedExpr) -> OptimizedExpr {
    match expr {
        OptimizedExpr::Choice(lhs, rhs) => coalesce_choice(*lhs, *rhs),
        OptimizedExpr::Seq(lhs, rhs) => coalesce_seq(*lhs, *rhs),
        OptimizedExpr::PosPred(inner) => OptimizedExpr::PosPred(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner))),
        OptimizedExpr::NegPred(inner) => OptimizedExpr::NegPred(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner))),
        OptimizedExpr::Rep(inner) => OptimizedExpr::Rep(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner))),
        OptimizedExpr::Opt(inner) => OptimizedExpr::Opt(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner))),
        OptimizedExpr::Push(inner) => OptimizedExpr::Push(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner))),
        #[cfg(feature = "grammar-extras")]
        OptimizedExpr::RepOnce(inner) => OptimizedExpr::RepOnce(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner))),
        #[cfg(feature = "grammar-extras")]
        OptimizedExpr::NodeTag(inner, tag) => {
            OptimizedExpr::NodeTag(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner)), tag)
        }
        OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(inner) => {
            OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*inner)))
        }
        expr => expr,
    }
}
```

Wait, there's a subtle issue: `coalesce_choice` calls `coalesce_expr` on each remaining alternative. But those alternatives are leaves
(Str/Range/CharClass/Insens/Ident/etc.) or wrappers like Opt/Rep/Push/RestoreOnErr. For wrappers, `coalesce_expr` recurses. Good.

But hold on: `coalesce_choice` itself is called from `coalesce_expr` for a Choice. After `coalesce_choice` rebuilds and recurses into alternatives, it
does NOT re-process the rebuilt top-level Choice as a whole. That's correct for top-down (we already processed this node; we don't re-coalesce after
children change). But consider a scenario: alternatives [a, b, x] where a,b,x are non-qualifying wrappers `Opt(Choice(...))`. Initially none qualify
-> no coalescing. Then we recurse into each; their inner choices become CharClass. But the outer Choice remains a Choice of three `Opt(CharClass)`
alternatives — no further coalescing, which is correct because they're still not qualifying (Opt isn't qualifying).

Another scenario: alternatives [a, b, RestoreOnErr(Choice(...))] where the RestoreOnErr contains a choice that after recursion becomes a single Str.
Initially `ranges_of(RestoreOnErr(Choice(...)))` returns None (inner Choice isn't qualifying leaf), so not part of a qualifying run. After recursion,
it becomes `RestoreOnErr(Str('c'))` which is now qualifying. But we already passed that run; no re-coalesce. This is an edge case unlikely in real
usage. Acceptable.

Now, another consideration: What if after recursing into alternatives, the result has only one alternative (e.g., original Choice had 2, both
coalesced into one Range)? `rebuild_choice` returns the single expr directly. Good.

Now, `ranges_of` signature: takes `&OptimizedExpr -> Option<Vec<(char,char)>>`. Implementation:

```rust
fn ranges_of(expr: &OptimizedExpr) -> Option<Vec<(char, char)>> {
    match expr {
        OptimizedExpr::Str(s) if s.chars().count() == 1 => {
            let c = s.chars().next().unwrap();
            Some(vec![(c, c)])
        }
        OptimizedExpr::Insens(s) if s.chars().count() == 1 => {
            let c = s.chars().next().unwrap();
            if c.is_ascii_alphabetic() {
                Some(vec![
                    (c.to_ascii_lowercase(), c.to_ascii_lowercase()),
                    (c.to_ascii_uppercase(), c.to_ascii_uppercase()),
                ])
            } else {
                Some(vec![(c, c)])
            }
        }
        OptimizedExpr::Range(start, end)
            if start.chars().count() == 1 && end.chars().count() == 1 =>
        {
            Some(vec![(
                start.chars().next().unwrap(),
                end.chars().next().unwrap(),
            )])
        }
        OptimizedExpr::CharClass(ranges) => {
            let mut result = Vec::with_capacity(ranges.len());
            for (start, end) in ranges {
                if start.chars().count() != 1 || end.chars().count() != 1 {
                    return None;
                }
                result.push((start.chars().next().unwrap(), end.chars().next().unwrap()));
            }
            Some(result)
        }
        OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(inner) => ranges_of(inner),
        _ => None,
    }
}
```

Note: the `Range` match arm has a guard; but if guard fails, falls through to `_ => None`. Good.

Now, `merge_ranges`:
```rust
fn merge_ranges(ranges: Vec<(char, char)>) -> Vec<(char, char)> {
    let mut ranges = ranges;
    ranges.sort_by_key(|(start, _)| *start as u32);
    let mut merged: Vec<(char, char)> = Vec::new();
    for (start, end) in ranges {
        if let Some((_, last_end)) = merged.last_mut() {
            if *last_end as u32 + 1 >= start as u32 {
                if end > *last_end {
                    *last_end = end;
                }
                continue;
            }
        }
        merged.push((start, end));
    }
    merged
}
```

This handles overlap and adjacency (start <= last_end+1). Since inclusive ranges, `last_end + 1 >= start` means overlap or adjacent. Good.

Edge: if `last_end` is `\u{10FFFF}`, `last_end as u32 + 1 = 0x110000` fits u32. Fine.

Now, `build_char_expr`:
```rust
fn build_char_expr(ranges: Vec<(char, char)>) -> Option<OptimizedExpr> {
    match ranges.as_slice() {
        [] => None,
        [(start, end)] => {
            if start == end {
                Some(OptimizedExpr::Str(start.to_string()))
            } else {
                Some(OptimizedExpr::Range(start.to_string(), end.to_string()))
            }
        }
        _ => Some(OptimizedExpr::CharClass(
            ranges
                .into_iter()
                .map(|(start, end)| (start.to_string(), end.to_string()))
                .collect(),
        )),
    }
}
```

Now, `to_string_range`:
```rust
fn to_string_ranges(ranges: Vec<(char, char)>) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
    ranges.into_iter().map(|(s, e)| (s.to_string(), e.to_string())).collect()
}
```

Now, let's think about the `optimize()` pipeline wiring. The coalescer runs after restorer. But wait — the `restorer` uses `optimized_map` built from
optimized rules before restorer. The coalescer doesn't need the map. So:

```rust
optimized
    .into_iter()
    .map(|rule| restorer::restore_on_err(rule, &optimized_map))
    .map(coalescer::coalesce)
    .collect()
```

Now, there's a potential issue: `CharClass`/`NegCharClass` variants in the `to_hash_map!` and `iter_top_down` etc. — the `iter_top_down` wildcard
handles them. `map_top_down`/`map_bottom_up` wildcard handles them. Good.

But wait: `map_bottom_up` and `map_top_down` match `OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr` in their `expr => expr` fallback (not recursing). Our coalescer uses
its own recursion, so fine.

Now, let's also add `CharClass`/`NegCharClass` handling to generator and vm.

Generator changes:
- In `generate_expr` and `generate_expr_atomic`, add arms for `CharClass(ranges)` and `NegCharClass(ranges)`.

Let me write a helper function in generator to produce the ranges literal:

```rust
fn char_ranges(ranges: Vec<(String, String)>) -> Vec<TokenStream> {
    ranges.iter().map(|(start, end)| {
        let start = start.chars().next().unwrap();
        let end = end.chars().next().unwrap();
        quote! { (#start, #end) }
    }).collect()
}
```

But generator.rs imports quote::quote. Let me check top of generator.rs for imports.

import re
path = 'meta/src/optimizer/mod.rs'
src = open(path).read()

# Insert CharClass and NegCharClass variants after Range variant
old = """    /// Matches one character in the range, e.g. `'a'..'z'`
    Range(String, String),
    /// Matches the rule with the given name, e.g. `a`
    Ident(String),"""
new = """    /// Matches one character in the range, e.g. `'a'..'z'`
    Range(String, String),
    /// Matches a single character contained in any of the ranges, e.g. `('a'..'z' | '0'..'9')`
    CharClass(Vec<(String, String)>),
    /// Matches a single character not contained in any of the ranges, e.g. `!('a'..'z') ~ ANY`
    NegCharClass(Vec<(String, String)>),
    /// Matches the rule with the given name, e.g. `a`
    Ident(String),"""

cd /app && cat > meta/src/optimizer/coalescer.rs <<'EOF'
// pest. The Elegant Parser
// Copyright (c) 2018 Dragoș Tiselice
//
// Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0
// <LICENSE-APACHE or http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0> or the MIT
// license <LICENSE-MIT or http://opensource.org/licenses/MIT>, at your
// option. All files in the project carrying such notice may not be copied,
// modified, or distributed except according to those terms.

use crate::optimizer::*;

pub fn coalesce(rule: OptimizedRule) -> OptimizedRule {
    let OptimizedRule { name, ty, expr } = rule;
    OptimizedRule {
        name,
        ty,
        expr: coalesce_expr(expr),
    }
}

fn coalesce_expr(expr: OptimizedExpr) -> OptimizedExpr {
    match expr {
        OptimizedExpr::Choice(lhs, rhs) => coalesce_choice(*lhs, *rhs),
        OptimizedExpr::Seq(lhs, rhs) => coalesce_seq(*lhs, *rhs),
        OptimizedExpr::PosPred(expr) => OptimizedExpr::PosPred(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
        OptimizedExpr::NegPred(expr) => OptimizedExpr::NegPred(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
        OptimizedExpr::Rep(expr) => OptimizedExpr::Rep(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
        OptimizedExpr::Opt(expr) => OptimizedExpr::Opt(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
        OptimizedExpr::Push(expr) => OptimizedExpr::Push(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
        #[cfg(feature = "grammar-extras")]
        OptimizedExpr::RepOnce(expr) => OptimizedExpr::RepOnce(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr))),
        #[cfg(feature = "grammar-extras")]
        OptimizedExpr::NodeTag(expr, tag) => {
            OptimizedExpr::NodeTag(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr)), tag)
        }
        OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(expr) => {
            OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(Box::new(coalesce_expr(*expr)))
        }
        expr => expr,
    }
}

fn coalesce_choice(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> OptimizedExpr {
    let alternatives = flatten_choice(lhs, rhs);
    let qualified: Vec<Option<Vec<(char, char)>>> =
        alternatives.iter().map(ranges_of).collect();

    let mut result: Vec<OptimizedExpr> = Vec::new();
    let mut i = 0;
    while i < alternatives.len() {
        if qualified[i].is_some() {
            let mut j = i;
            while j < alternatives.len() && qualified[j].is_some() {
                j += 1;
            }

            let run_len = j - i;
            let all_qualified = i == 0 && j == alternatives.len();
            let ranges: Vec<(char, char)> = (i..j)
                .flat_map(|k| qualified[k].as_ref().expect("qualified").iter().copied())
                .collect();
            let merged = merge_ranges(ranges);

            let coalesce = if all_qualified {
                merged.len() < run_len
            } else {
                run_len >= 3 && merged.len() < run_len
            };

            if coalesce {
                match build_char_expr(merged) {
                    Some(expr) => result.push(expr),
                    None => result.extend(alternatives[i..j].iter().cloned()),
                }
            } else {
                result.extend(alternatives[i..j].iter().cloned());
            }

            i = j;
        } else {
            result.push(alternatives[i].clone());
            i += 1;
        }
    }

    let result: Vec<OptimizedExpr> = result.into_iter().map(coalesce_expr).collect();
    rebuild_choice(result)
}

fn coalesce_seq(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> OptimizedExpr {
    if let OptimizedExpr::NegPred(expr) = &lhs {
        if let OptimizedExpr::Ident(name) = &rhs {
            if name == "ANY" {
                if let Some(ranges) = collect_neg_ranges(expr) {
                    let merged = merge_ranges(ranges);
                    if !merged.is_empty() {
                        return OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(to_string_ranges(merged));
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    OptimizedExpr::Seq(
        Box::new(coalesce_expr(lhs)),
        Box::new(coalesce_expr(rhs)),
    )
}

fn flatten_choice(lhs: OptimizedExpr, rhs: OptimizedExpr) -> Vec<OptimizedExpr> {
    let mut alternatives = vec![lhs];
    let mut current = rhs;
    while let OptimizedExpr::Choice(l, r) = current {
        alternatives.push(*l);
        current = *r;
    }
    alternatives.push(current);
    alternatives
}

fn rebuild_choice(alternatives: Vec<OptimizedExpr>) -> OptimizedExpr {
    let mut iter = alternatives.into_iter().rev();
    let mut acc = iter.next().expect("non-empty choice");
    for alt in iter {
        acc = OptimizedExpr::Choice(Box::new(alt), Box::new(acc));
    }
    acc
}

fn collect_neg_ranges(expr: &OptimizedExpr) -> Option<Vec<(char, char)>> {
    let mut alternatives = Vec::new();
    let mut current = expr;
    while let OptimizedExpr::Choice(lhs, rhs) = current {
        alternatives.push(lhs.as_ref());
        current = rhs.as_ref();
    }
    alternatives.push(current);

    let mut ranges = Vec::new();
    for alt in alternatives {
        ranges.extend(ranges_of(alt)?);
    }
    Some(ranges)
}

fn ranges_of(expr: &OptimizedExpr) -> Option<Vec<(char, char)>> {
    match expr {
        OptimizedExpr::Str(string) if string.chars().count() == 1 => {
            let c = string.chars().next().expect("single char");
            Some(vec![(c, c)])
        }
        OptimizedExpr::Insens(string) if string.chars().count() == 1 => {
            let c = string.chars().next().expect("single char");
            if c.is_ascii_alphabetic() {
                Some(vec![
                    (c.to_ascii_lowercase(), c.to_ascii_lowercase()),
                    (c.to_ascii_uppercase(), c.to_ascii_uppercase()),
                ])
            } else {
                Some(vec![(c, c)])
            }
        }
        OptimizedExpr::Range(start, end)
            if start.chars().count() == 1 && end.chars().count() == 1 =>
        {
            Some(vec![(
                start.chars().next().expect("single char"),
                end.chars().next().expect("single char"),
            )])
        }
        OptimizedExpr::CharClass(ranges) => {
            let mut result = Vec::with_capacity(ranges.len());
            for (start, end) in ranges {
                if start.chars().count() != 1 || end.chars().count() != 1 {
                    return None;
                }
                result.push((
                    start.chars().next().expect("single char"),
                    end.chars().next().expect("single char"),
                ));
            }
            Some(result)
        }
        OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(expr) => ranges_of(expr),
        _ => None,
    }
}

fn merge_ranges(ranges: Vec<(char, char)>) -> Vec<(char, char)> {
    let mut ranges = ranges;
    ranges.sort_by_key(|(start, _)| *start as u32);

    let mut merged: Vec<(char, char)> = Vec::new();
    for (start, end) in ranges {
        if let Some((_, last_end)) = merged.last_mut() {
            if *last_end as u32 + 1 >= start as u32 {
                if end > *last_end {
                    *last_end = end;
                }
                continue;
            }
        }
        merged.push((start, end));
    }
    merged
}

fn build_char_expr(ranges: Vec<(char, char)>) -> Option<OptimizedExpr> {
    match ranges.as_slice() {
        [] => None,
        [(start, end)] => {
            if start == end {
                Some(OptimizedExpr::Str(start.to_string()))
            } else {
                Some(OptimizedExpr::Range(start.to_string(), end.to_string()))
            }
        }
        _ => Some(OptimizedExpr::CharClass(to_string_ranges(ranges))),
    }
}

fn to_string_ranges(ranges: Vec<(char, char)>) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
    ranges
        .into_iter()
        .map(|(start, end)| (start.to_string(), end.to_string()))
        .collect()
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn choice(alternatives: Vec<OptimizedExpr>) -> OptimizedExpr {
        rebuild_choice(alternatives)
    }

    fn str_(s: &str) -> OptimizedExpr {
        OptimizedExpr::Str(s.to_owned())
    }

    fn range(a: &str, b: &str) -> OptimizedExpr {
        OptimizedExpr::Range(a.to_owned(), b.to_owned())
    }

    #[test]
    fn full_choice_collapses_to_range() {
        assert_eq!(
            coalesce_expr(choice(vec![str_("a"), str_("b"), str_("c")])),
            range("a", "c")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn full_choice_collapses_to_str() {
        assert_eq!(
            coalesce_expr(choice(vec![str_("a"), range("b", "c")])),
            range("a", "c")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn full_choice_not_emitted_when_not_fewer() {
        let original = choice(vec![str_("a"), str_("z"), str_("x")]);
        assert_eq!(coalesce_expr(original.clone()), original);
    }

    #[test]
    fn partial_runs_of_three() {
        let original = choice(vec![
            str_("a"),
            OptimizedExpr::Ident("x".to_owned()),
            str_("b"),
            str_("c"),
            str_("d"),
        ]);
        let expected = choice(vec![
            str_("a"),
            OptimizedExpr::Ident("x".to_owned()),
            range("b", "d"),
        ]);
        assert_eq!(coalesce_expr(original), expected);
    }

    #[test]
    fn partial_run_of_two_not_coalesced() {
        let original = choice(vec![
            str_("a"),
            OptimizedExpr::Ident("x".to_owned()),
            str_("b"),
            str_("c"),
        ]);
        let expected = choice(vec![
            str_("a"),
            OptimizedExpr::Ident("x".to_owned()),
            range("b", "c"),
        ]);
        assert_eq!(coalesce_expr(original), expected);
    }

    #[test]
    fn restore_on_err_wrapper_stripped() {
        assert_eq!(
            coalesce_expr(choice(vec![
                OptimizedExpr::RestoreOnErr(Box::new(str_("a"))),
                str_("b"),
                str_("c"),
            ])),
            range("a", "c")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn insens_expands_cases() {
        assert_eq!(
            coalesce_expr(choice(vec![
                OptimizedExpr::Insens("a".to_owned()),
                str_("b"),
                str_("c"),
            ])),
            OptimizedExpr::CharClass(vec![
                ("A".to_owned(), "A".to_owned()),
                ("a".to_owned(), "c".to_owned()),
            ])
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn overlapping_and_adjacent_ranges_merge() {
        assert_eq!(
            coalesce_expr(choice(vec![
                range("a", "c"),
                range("b", "e"),
                range("f", "g"),
            ])),
            range("a", "g")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn neg_pred_any_collapses() {
        let original = OptimizedExpr::Seq(
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::NegPred(Box::new(choice(vec![
                str_("a"),
                str_("b"),
                str_("c"),
            ])))),
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Ident("ANY".to_owned())),
        );
        assert_eq!(
            coalesce_expr(original),
            OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(vec![("a".to_owned(), "c".to_owned())])
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn neg_pred_any_single_range() {
        let original = OptimizedExpr::Seq(
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::NegPred(Box::new(range("a", "z")))),
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Ident("ANY".to_owned())),
        );
        assert_eq!(
            coalesce_expr(original),
            OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(vec![("a".to_owned(), "z".to_owned())])
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn neg_pred_partial_not_collapsed() {
        let original = OptimizedExpr::Seq(
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::NegPred(Box::new(choice(vec![
                str_("a"),
                OptimizedExpr::Ident("x".to_owned()),
            ])))),
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Ident("ANY".to_owned())),
        );
        assert_eq!(coalesce_expr(original.clone()), original);
    }
}

```rust
    /// Attempts to match a single character contained in any of the given ranges. Returns `Ok` with
    /// the updated `Box<ParserState>` if successful, or `Err` with the updated `Box<ParserState>`
    /// otherwise. The ranges are interpreted as inclusive.
    ///
    /// # Examples
    /// ...
    #[inline]
    pub fn match_char_class(mut self: Box<Self>, ranges: &[(char, char)]) -> ParseResult<Box<Self>> {
        let start_position = self.position.pos();
        let succeeded = self.position.match_char_by(|c| {
            ranges.iter().any(|(start, end)| start <= &c && &c <= end)
        });
        if self.parse_attempts.enabled {
            let token = ParsingToken::BuiltInRule;
            self.handle_token_parse_result(start_position, token, succeeded);
        }
        if succeeded {
            Ok(self)
        } else {
            Err(self)
        }
    }

    /// Attempts to match a single character not contained in any of the given ranges. ...
    pub fn match_neg_char_class(mut self: Box<Self>, ranges: &[(char, char)]) -> ParseResult<Box<Self>> {
        let start_position = self.position.pos();
        let succeeded = self.position.match_char_by(|c| {
            !ranges.iter().any(|(start, end)| start <= &c && &c <= end)
        });
        if self.parse_attempts.enabled {
            let token = ParsingToken::BuiltInRule;
            self.handle_token_parse_result(start_position, token, succeeded);
        }
        if succeeded {
            Ok(self)
        } else {
            Err(self)
        }
    }
```

new = """    /// Attempts to match a single character contained in any of the given ranges. Returns `Ok` with
    /// the updated `Box<ParserState>` if successful, or `Err` with the updated `Box<ParserState>`
    /// otherwise. The ranges are interpreted as inclusive.
    ///
    /// # Examples
    ///
    /// ```
    /// # use pest;
    /// # #[allow(non_camel_case_types)]
    /// # #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, Hash, Ord, PartialEq, PartialOrd)]
    /// enum Rule {}
    ///
    /// let input = "ab";
    /// let mut state: Box<pest::ParserState<'_, Rule>> = pest::ParserState::new(input);
    /// let mut result = state.match_char_class(&[('a', 'c')]);
    /// assert!(result.is_ok());
    /// assert_eq!(result.unwrap().position().pos(), 1);
    ///
    /// state = pest::ParserState::new(input);
    /// result = state.match_char_class(&[('x', 'z')]);
    /// assert!(result.is_err());
    /// assert_eq!(result.unwrap_err().position().pos(), 0);
    /// ```
    #[inline]
    pub fn match_char_class(
        mut self: Box<Self>,
        ranges: &[(char, char)],
    ) -> ParseResult<Box<Self>> {
        let start_position = self.position.pos();
        let succeeded = self.position.match_char_by(|c| {
            ranges.iter().any(|(start, end)| start <= &c && &c <= end)
        });
        if self.parse_attempts.enabled {
            let token = ParsingToken::BuiltInRule;
            self.handle_token_parse_result(start_position, token, succeeded);
        }
        if succeeded {
            Ok(self)
        } else {
            Err(self)
        }
    }

    /// Attempts to match a single character not contained in any of the given ranges. Returns `Ok`
    /// with the updated `Box<ParserState>` if successful, or `Err` with the updated
    /// `Box<ParserState>` otherwise. The ranges are interpreted as inclusive.
    ///
    /// # Examples
    ///
    /// ```
    /// # use pest;
    /// # #[allow(non_camel_case_types)]
    /// # #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, Hash, Ord, PartialEq, PartialOrd)]
    /// enum Rule {}
    ///
    /// let input = "ab";
    /// let mut state: Box<pest::ParserState<'_, Rule>> = pest::ParserState::new(input);
    /// let mut result = state.match_neg_char_class(&[('b', 'z')]);
    /// assert!(result.is_ok());
    /// assert_eq!(result.unwrap().position().pos(), 1);
    ///
    /// state = pest::ParserState::new(input);
    /// result = state.match_neg_char_class(&[('a', 'c')]);
    /// assert!(result.is_err());
    /// assert_eq!(result.unwrap_err().position().pos(), 0);
    /// ```
    #[inline]
    pub fn match_neg_char_class(
        mut self: Box<Self>,
        ranges: &[(char, char)],
    ) -> ParseResult<Box<Self>> {
        let start_position = self.position.pos();
        let succeeded = self.position.match_char_by(|c| {
            !ranges.iter().any(|(start, end)| start <= &c && &c <= end)
        });
        if self.parse_attempts.enabled {
            let token = ParsingToken::BuiltInRule;
            self.handle_token_parse_result(start_position, token, succeeded);
        }
        if succeeded {
            Ok(self)
        } else {
            Err(self)
        }
    }

    /// Attempts to skip `n` characters forward. Returns `Ok` with the updated `Box<ParserState>`
    /// if successful, or `Err` with the updated `Box<ParserState>` otherwise.
"""
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)
open(path, 'w').write(src)
print("methods inserted")
PY

Let me add helper:

```rust
fn char_class_ranges(ranges: Vec<(String, String)>) -> Vec<TokenStream> {
    ranges
        .into_iter()
        .map(|(start, end)| {
            let start = start.chars().next().unwrap();
            let end = end.chars().next().unwrap();
            quote! { (#start, #end) }
        })
        .collect()
}
```

Then in generate_expr:
```rust
OptimizedExpr::CharClass(ranges) => {
    let ranges = char_class_ranges(ranges);
    quote! {
        state.match_char_class(&[#(#ranges),*])
    }
}
OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(ranges) => {
    let ranges = char_class_ranges(ranges);
    quote! {
        state.match_neg_char_class(&[#(#ranges),*])
    }
}
```

```rust
OptimizedExpr::CharClass(ref ranges) => {
    let ranges = ranges
        .iter()
        .map(|(start, end)| {
            (start.chars().next().expect("empty char literal"), end.chars().next().expect("empty char literal"))
        })
        .collect::<Vec<_>>();
    state.match_char_class(&ranges)
}
OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(ref ranges) => {
    let ranges = ...;
    state.match_neg_char_class(&ranges)
}
```

extern crate pest;
extern crate pest_meta;
#[macro_use]
extern crate pest_vm;

use pest_meta::parser::Rule;
use pest_meta::{optimizer, parser};
use pest_vm::Vm;

const GRAMMAR: &str = include_str!("surround.pest");

fn vm() -> Vm {
    let pairs = parser::parse(Rule::grammar_rules, GRAMMAR).unwrap();
    let ast = parser::consume_rules(pairs).unwrap();
    Vm::new(optimizer::optimize(ast))
}

#[macro_use]
extern crate pest;
use pest_grammars::{http::*, Parser};

#[test]
fn method() {
    parses_to! {
        parser: HttpParser,
        input: "GET",
        rule: Rule::method,
        tokens: [
            method(0, 3)
        ]
    };
}

#[test]
fn uri() {
    parses_to! {
        parser: HttpParser,
        input: "/",
        rule: Rule::uri,
        tokens: [
            uri(0, 1)
        ]
    };
}

#[test]
fn version() {
    parses_to! {
        parser: HttpParser,
        input: "1.1",
        rule: Rule::version,
        tokens: [
            version(0, 3)
        ]
    };
}

#[test]
fn header() {
    parses_to! {
        parser: HttpParser,
        input: "Connection: keep-alive\n",
        rule: Rule::header,
        tokens: [
            header(0, 23, [
                header_name(0, 10),
                header_value(12, 22),
            ])
        ]
    };
}

#[test]
fn examples() {
    let data = include_str!(concat!(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"), "/tests/examples.http"));
    assert!(HttpParser::parse(Rule::http, data).is_ok());
}

// pest. The Elegant Parser

extern crate pest;
extern crate pest_meta;
#[macro_use]
extern crate pest_vm;

use pest_meta::parser::Rule;
use pest_meta::{optimizer, parser};
use pest_vm::Vm;

const GRAMMAR: &str = include_str!("reporting.pest");

fn vm() -> Vm {
    let pairs = parser::parse(Rule::grammar_rules, GRAMMAR).unwrap();
    let ast = parser::consume_rules(pairs).unwrap();
    Vm::new(optimizer::optimize(ast))
}

#[test]
fn choices() {
    fails_with! {
        parser: vm(),
        input: "x",
        rule: "choices",
        positives: vec!["a", "b", "c"],
        negatives: vec![],
        pos: 0
    };
}

#[test]
fn choices_no_progress() {
    fails_with! {
        parser: vm(),
        input: "x",
        rule: "choices_no_progress",
        positives: vec!["choices_no_progress"],
        negatives: vec![],
        pos: 0
    };
}

// pest. The Elegant Parser

// pest. The Elegant Parser
// Licensed under MIT/Apache-2.0

extern crate pest;
extern crate pest_meta;
#[macro_use]
extern crate pest_vm;

use pest_meta::optimizer::{OptimizedExpr, OptimizedRule};
use pest_meta::parser::Rule;
use pest_meta::{optimizer, parser};
use pest_vm::Vm;

const GRAMMAR: &str = include_str!("coalescing.pest");

fn optimized() -> Vec<OptimizedRule> {
    let pairs = parser::parse(Rule::grammar_rules, GRAMMAR).unwrap();
    let ast = parser::consume_rules(pairs).unwrap();
    optimizer::optimize(ast)
}

#[test]
fn full_range_collapses() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let rule = rules.iter().find(|r| r.name == "full_range").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        rule.expr,
        OptimizedExpr::Range("a".to_owned(), "c".to_owned())
    );
}

#[test]
fn char_class_is_produced() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let rule = rules.iter().find(|r| r.name == "two_ranges").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        rule.expr,
        OptimizedExpr::CharClass(vec![
            ("a".to_owned(), "c".to_owned()),
            ("x".to_owned(), "z".to_owned()),
        ])
    );
}

#[test]
fn partial_run_is_coalesced() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let rule = rules.iter().find(|r| r.name == "partial").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        rule.expr,
        OptimizedExpr::Choice(
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Str("a".to_owned())),
            Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Choice(
                Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Ident("ident".to_owned())),
                Box::new(OptimizedExpr::Range("b".to_owned(), "d".to_owned())),
            )),
        )
    );
}

#[test]
fn neg_char_class_is_produced() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let rule = rules.iter().find(|r| r.name == "negated").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        rule.expr,
        OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(vec![("a".to_owned(), "c".to_owned())])
    );
}

#[test]
fn neg_char_class_single_is_produced() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let rule = rules.iter().find(|r| r.name == "negated_single").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        rule.expr,
        OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(vec![("x".to_owned(), "x".to_owned())])
    );
}

#[test]
fn vm_parses_char_class() {
    let rules = optimized();
    let vm = Vm::new(rules);
    assert!(vm.parse("full_range", "a").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("full_range", "c").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("full_range", "d").is_err());

    assert!(vm.parse("two_ranges", "b").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("two_ranges", "y").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("two_ranges", "m").is_err());

    assert!(vm.parse("partial", "a").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("partial", "c").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("partial", "foo").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("partial", "e").is_err());

    assert!(vm.parse("negated", "d").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("negated", "a").is_err());
    assert!(vm.parse("negated_single", "a").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("negated_single", "x").is_err());

    assert!(vm.parse("mixed", "A").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("mixed", "a").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("mixed", "d").is_ok());
    assert!(vm.parse("mixed", "e").is_err());
}
EOF

So `character` (single-quoted) only appears in `range`; there's no standalone character terminal. To express a single character alternative in pest
grammar, you use `"a"` (a one-char string). So my grammar should use `"a" | "b" | "c"` instead of `'a' | 'b' | 'c'`. That's fine — Str("a") is
single-char and qualifies. Let me update the .pest file.

path = 'generator/src/generator.rs'
src = open(path).read()

old = """    #[test]
    fn skip() {
        let expr = OptimizedExpr::Skip(vec!["a".to_owned(), "b".to_owned()]);

new = """    #[test]
    fn char_class() {
        let expr = OptimizedExpr::CharClass(vec![
            ("a".to_owned(), "c".to_owned()),
            ("x".to_owned(), "z".to_owned()),
        ]);

        assert_eq!(

            quote! {

            }
            .to_string()
        );
    }

        let expr = OptimizedExpr::NegCharClass(vec![
            ("a".to_owned(), "c".to_owned()),
            ("x".to_owned(), "z".to_owned()),
        ]);

        assert_eq!(
            generate_expr(expr.clone()).to_string(),

            quote! {
                state.match_neg_char_class(&[('a', 'c'), ('x', 'z')])
            }
            .to_string()
        );

    #[test]
    fn skip() {
        let expr = OptimizedExpr::Skip(vec!["a".to_owned(), "b".to_owned()]);

//! # pest grammars
//!
//! Contains a series of default grammars.

#![doc(
    html_root_url = "https://docs.rs/pest_grammars",
    html_logo_url = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pest-parser/pest/master/pest-logo.svg",
    html_favicon_url = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pest-parser/pest/master/pest-logo.svg"
)]
#![warn(missing_docs, rust_2018_idioms, unused_qualifications)]

#[macro_use]
extern crate pest_derive;

pub use pest::Parser;

/// Grammar rules of a simplified HTTP request parser
#[allow(missing_docs)]
pub mod http {
    /// HTTP parser.
    #[derive(Parser)]
    #[grammar = "grammars/http.pest"]
    pub struct HttpParser;
}

/// Grammar rules of a sample JSON parser
#[allow(missing_docs)]
pub mod json {
    /// JSON parser.
    #[derive(Parser)]
    #[grammar = "grammars/json.pest"]
    pub struct JsonParser;
}

/// Grammar rules of a sample TOML parser
#[allow(missing_docs)]
pub mod toml {
    /// TOML parser.
    #[derive(Parser)]
    #[grammar = "grammars/toml.pest"]
    pub struct TomlParser;
}

/// Grammar rules of an SQL parser
#[allow(missing_docs)]
pub mod sql {
    /// SQL parser.
    /// Grammar is a tinkered version of the one used in distributed SQL executor module named
    /// [sbroad](https://git.picodata.io/picodata/picodata/sbroad/-/blob/main/sbroad-core/src/frontend/sql/query.pest).
    /// Being a submodule of [Picodata](https://git.picodata.io/picodata/picodata/picodata) (that
    /// operates with Tarantool database) it tries to simulate SQLite flavour (Tarantool uses
    /// SQLite to execute SQL queries).
    #[derive(Parser)]
    #[grammar = "grammars/sql.pest"]
    pub struct SqlParser;
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use pest::iterators::Pairs;
    use std::convert::TryInto;

    use pest::pratt_parser::PrattParser;
    use pest::Parser;

    use crate::{json, sql, toml};

    fn test_toml_deep_nesting(input: &str) {
        const ERROR: &str = "call limit reached";
        pest::set_call_limit(Some(5_000usize.try_into().unwrap()));
        let s = toml::TomlParser::parse(toml::Rule::toml, input);
        assert!(s.is_err());
        assert_eq!(s.unwrap_err().variant.message(), ERROR);
    }

    #[test]
    fn toml_handles_deep_nesting() {
        let sample1 = include_str!(concat!(
            env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"),
            "/resources/test/tomlfuzzsample1.toml"
        ));
        test_toml_deep_nesting(sample1);
    }

    #[test]
    #[ignore = "this sometimes crashes in the debug mode"]
    fn toml_handles_deep_nesting_unstable() {
        let sample2 = include_str!(concat!(
            env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"),
            "/resources/test/tomlfuzzsample2.toml"
        ));
        test_toml_deep_nesting(sample2);
    }

    #[test]
    fn json_handles_deep_nesting() {
        let sample1 = include_str!(concat!(
            env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"),
            "/resources/test/jsonfuzzsample1.json"
        ));
        let sample2 = include_str!(concat!(
            env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"),
            "/resources/test/jsonfuzzsample2.json"
        ));
        const ERROR: &str = "call limit reached";
        pest::set_call_limit(Some(5_000usize.try_into().unwrap()));
        let s1 = json::JsonParser::parse(json::Rule::json, sample1);
        assert!(s1.is_err());
        assert_eq!(s1.unwrap_err().variant.message(), ERROR);
        let s2 = json::JsonParser::parse(json::Rule::json, sample2);
        assert!(s2.is_err());
        assert_eq!(s2.unwrap_err().variant.message(), ERROR);
    }

    #[test]
    fn sql_check_expressions_priorities() {
        lazy_static::lazy_static! {
            static ref PRATT_PARSER: PrattParser<sql::Rule> = {
                use pest::pratt_parser::{Assoc::{Left, Right}, Op};
                use sql::Rule::{Add, And, Between, ConcatInfixOp, Divide, Eq, Gt, GtEq, In,
                                IsNullPostfix, Lt, LtEq, Multiply, NotEq, Or, Subtract, UnaryNot};

                // Precedence is defined lowest to highest.
                PrattParser::new()
                    .op(Op::infix(Or, Left))
                    .op(Op::infix(Between, Left))
                    .op(Op::infix(And, Left))
                    .op(Op::prefix(UnaryNot))
                    .op(
                        Op::infix(Eq, Right) | Op::infix(NotEq, Right) | Op::infix(NotEq, Right)
                            | Op::infix(Gt, Right) | Op::infix(GtEq, Right) | Op::infix(Lt, Right)
                            | Op::infix(LtEq, Right) | Op::infix(In, Right)
                    )
                    .op(Op::infix(Add, Left) | Op::in
output_tail:
 right: Box::new(Expr::ArithValue(2)),
                        }),
                    }),
                    op: InfixOp::BoolInfix(BoolOp::Eq),
                    right: Box::new(Expr::ArithValue(3)),
                }),
            }),
            right: Box::new(Expr::Infix {
                left: Box::new(Expr::IsNull {
                    child: Box::new(Expr::BoolConst(false)),
                }),
                op: InfixOp::BoolInfix(BoolOp::And),
                right: Box::new(Expr::Infix {
                    left: Box::new(Expr::ArithValue(1)),
                    op: InfixOp::BoolInfix(BoolOp::In),
                    right: Box::new(Expr::SubQuery),
                }),
            }),
        };
        assert_eq!(expected_expr, actual_expr);
    }

    #[test]
    fn sql_parse_attempts_error() {
        pest::set_error_detail(true);

        fn is_whitespace(string: String) -> bool {
            string == "\r\n"
                || (string.len() == 1 && string.chars().next().unwrap().is_whitespace())
        }

        fn rule_to_message(r: &sql::Rule) -> Option<String> {
            match r {
                sql::Rule::CreateTable => Some(String::from("Expected table creation.")),
                sql::Rule::PrimaryKey => Some(String::from(
                    "Add primary key consisting of non nullable table columns.",
                )),
                sql::Rule::CreateUser => Some(String::from("Expected user creation.")),
                sql::Rule::SingleQuotedString => {
                    Some(String::from("Add a string in single quotes."))
                }
                sql::Rule::Query => Some(String::from("DML query expected.")),
                sql::Rule::Expr => Some(String::from("Expected expression.")),
                _ => None,
            }
        }

        type RuleToMessageBoxed = Box<dyn Fn(&sql::Rule) -> Option<String>>;
        type IsWhiteSpaceBoxed = Box<dyn Fn(String) -> bool>;

        let rule_to_message_boxed: RuleToMessageBoxed = Box::new(rule_to_message);
        let is_whitespace_boxed: IsWhiteSpaceBoxed = Box::new(is_whitespace);

        let retrieve_parse_attempts_error_string = |input| {
            let e = sql::SqlParser::parse(sql::Rule::Command, input).unwrap_err();
            let parse_attempt_error = e
                .parse_attempts_error(input, &rule_to_message_boxed, &is_whitespace_boxed)
                .unwrap();
            format!("{parse_attempt_error}")
        };

        let table_creation_without_primary_key =
            r#"create table t(col_1 int,) distributed by (col_1)"#;
        assert_eq!(
            retrieve_parse_attempts_error_string(table_creation_without_primary_key),
            [
                " --> 1:26",
                "  |",
                "1 | create table t(col_1 int,) distributed by (col_1)",
                "  |                          ^---",
                "  |",
                "  = error: parsing error occurred.",
                r#"    note: expected one of tokens: WHITESPACE, `"`, `-`, `A..Z`, `PRIMARY`, `_`, `a..z`, `А..Я`, `а..я`"#,
                "    help: Expected table creation.",
                "          - Add primary key consisting of non nullable table columns.",
            ]
            .join("\n")
        );

        let user_creation_password_without_single_quotes = r#"create user
                                                                   Bob password "wrong""#;
        assert_eq!(
            retrieve_parse_attempts_error_string(user_creation_password_without_single_quotes),
            [
                " --> 2:81",
                "  |",
                r#"2 |                                                                    Bob password "wrong""#,
                "  |                                                                                 ^---",
                "  |",
                "  = error: parsing error occurred.",
                "    note: expected one of tokens: WHITESPACE, `''`, `'`",
                "    help: Expected user creation.",
                "          - Add a string in single quotes.",
            ]
                .join("\n")
        );

        let invalid_expression_in_projection = r#"select 1 + from t"#;
        assert_eq!(
            retrieve_parse_attempts_error_string(invalid_expression_in_projection),
            [
                " --> 1:12",
                "  |",
                "1 | select 1 + from t",
                "  |            ^---",
                "  |",
                "  = error: parsing error occurred.",
                r#"    note: expected one of tokens: WHITESPACE, `"`, `$`, `''`, `'`, `(`, `+`, `-`, `0..9`, `?`, `CAST`, `EXISTS`, `FALSE`, `NOT`, `NULL`, `TRUE`"#,
                "    note: unexpected token: `FROM`",
                "    help: DML query expected.",
                "          - Expected expression.",
            ]
                .join("\n")
        );
    }
}
